🎧 Audio Brief ↗
Pre-Scan Snapshot Scan 2 Pending
🔁11 of 12 assigned sessions completed (91.7%), updated through tonight's final pre-scan session. Three lifting days a week on top of tennis Mon, Tue and Thu, at 77. The one gap, Sun Sep 6, he called himself after back soreness and a low deep-sleep reading from his watch. That is self-regulation, not a lapse, and it should be named as good judgement in the room.
🏆Lat pulldown is genuinely solved and has now beaten the week-out target. Sep 15: 67.5 for 12, 12, 12. Sep 17: 72.5, meeting target. Tonight, Sep 22: 75 for 12, 11, 8, exceeding the 72.5 ask. Real progress to open with.
🍌He implemented the carb change the day after the call. His own Sep 9 food-journal note: "Had a banana and shake before weight training and a shake and a cracker after." The 10-day call was Sep 8. He acts the same week he is told something, every time.
📓He logged exactly what he was asked to log. 40 food-journal entries, Aug 28 through Sep 10. Daniel asked on Sep 8 for "just two more days"; he delivered Sep 9 and Sep 10 and stopped. Do not read the stop as a drop-off, it is compliance with the instruction he was given.
🧠He responds to reasons, not directives. The cortisol and recovery explanation on the Sep 8 call is what got the carbs in. He raised the alternative-exercise problem himself in August, sent photographs of his gym unprompted, and uses wearable sleep data to make his own load decisions.
Two things to settle before or at the scan:
The Sep 13 DM is Seen and unanswered. It asked directly about deep sleep and set three things to hold for the week. His last message in the thread is "Thanks" on Aug 31. He is not a thread replier, four short messages in a month, and he communicates through food-journal notes instead. Read silence accordingly, and ask the sleep question out loud.
Shoulder press took the correction, then lost it again: check in, don't assume it's fixed. Sep 15 was 30 for 7, 6, 6. Sep 17 he took the correction cleanly, 30 for 12, then 30 for 10. Tonight, Sep 22, he regressed to 30 for 7, 5 at the same weight. Ask what's going on rather than repeat last week's line; the shoulder arthritis and 25% imbalance make this the safety lift.
New since the week-out targets: a left hip tweak that stalled his RDL, unaddressed. A Sep 13 workout comment flagged it and nobody followed up; RDL was Missed Sep 13 and Sep 17, then resumed tonight at 60 lb for 13/12, well under the 75 lb target. Ask about the hip directly in the room.

Their why, in full Longevity and independence. Stay capable and active, "I like to keep moving", to 90+. 🎯 Goals & Motivation
Goal: lean mass gain. Source is his ALMI, not an agreement ("ALMI 4th, well under 60th"). It happens to match what he says he wants, but get him to own it out loud rather than asserting it at him.
Current focus: 3x/week lifting, up from 2, with progressive overload close to failure. Build muscle to raise ALMI and bring visceral fat down through protein, green tea, microgreens, dark berries and Greek yogurt. Carb timing around the tennis-plus-weights days for energy, recovery and cortisol, added Sep 8.
Why: longevity and independence. Staying capable and active, "I like to keep moving", into his nineties. He took the low ALMI percentile at the intro scan and turned it into a concrete target himself. Muscle framed as insurance against functional decline. Iris rates the why specific and anchored, so it is safe to lean on directly.
The why is strong but it has no scene in it. "Capable at 90" is a state, not a picture. The 6-to-12-month question has never been asked of him and it is the right tool. His answer is the most valuable thing to leave the room with.
Personal Context & Coaching Flags
Renewal closes in the room. Iris: Membership 4 Week, start Jun 24, end Sep 23. The search row says "No current package". There is no cliff after this conversation, there is only this conversation.
Visceral fat is the one number moving the wrong way. 92.5 cm² at the Jun 24 intro, 109.0 cm² on Aug 26, up 17.8%. The Canada and Europe trip sits in the middle of that window and he named it himself. The Gut Fire protocol went out Aug 27, so this scan is the first read on whether it moved. He travels 40 to 50 days a year, which makes this a structural question rather than a one-off.
Phase change landed six days before the scan. Foundation P1 → P2 on Sep 17. P2 asks 12 reps at 60 s rest on every set, so loads that were built for lower reps need to come down or the sets fade. He has A on Sep 17 and again Sep 22, B on Sep 19. Sep 22 is the last session before the scan.
Shoulders are the injury risk, and they are the lift still misbehaving. Shoulder and knee arthritis, right-hand weakness limiting pressing, and a 25% right-left arm imbalance that raises injury risk on overhead work, possibly from tennis. Standing instruction: lighter and nearer 12 reps when fatigued. Sep 3 he did 15 for 4, 4, 4, which is exactly the pattern Daniel called dangerous on the Sep 8 call.
CORRECTED: that Sep 13 comment was a real limiter, not a self-log. "I tweaked my left hip somehow last session... skipped the deadlifting and I'm not pushing very hard today." Nobody in the coaching thread followed up. His DB RDL was marked Missed Sep 13 and again Sep 17, then resumed tonight (Sep 22) at 60 lb for 13/12, clean reps but well under the 75 lb target and lighter than a month ago. Ask about the hip directly in the room.
The equipment note on file is overstated and should be corrected. Iris and the member record both say "limited to dumbbells + bodyweight at Saratoga club (no machines)". His own logs contradict that: he is putting real weight through Seated Leg Press, Lat Pulldown, Chest Press Machine and Cable Seated Row every week. What he actually lacked in August was a Smith machine and a hamstring curl, and Daniel reprogrammed the alternatives on Aug 29. P2 leans on leg press, lat pulldown and cables, and he can do all of it.
Sleep is his own instrument. He reads deep sleep off his watch and adjusts training load from it. That is the lever that gets him to change behaviour, and it is why the carb argument landed. Daniel's Sep 13 question about deep sleep is still unanswered.
Life. Tennis Mon, Tue and Thu, weights straight after tennis on those days, so training lands around 11 AM or noon. Family-style Chinese meals, low appetite, gout under control. Birthday Aug 29, he turned 77 three days after the baseline scan.

Gold Standards, Male 60+, 167 lb: bench ~165, squat ~210, deadlift ~250. Use the bench one, leave the other two alone. He is already over the bench standard, which is a genuinely good thing to tell a 63-year-old four weeks in. The squat and deadlift standards are not useful here: he has a hip he is protecting by choice, he is deliberately short of full depth, and P2 has removed the barbell deadlift from his program entirely, so a 250 lb target is an abstraction. Use direction instead: squat 95 → 115 and bench 145 → 165 in two weeks, sit-ups from failing at set three to holding load across five, and ALMI 23rd percentile climbing toward the 60th, which is the benchmark that actually matters for him.
Ask before you prescribe on the travel numbers. A London gym may simply not have had the increments, and 15 reps at a fixed weight can be a plate problem rather than an effort problem. The pyramid on the leg press and the shoulder press is the harder evidence, because both happened inside one session with the same equipment available.

Body fat 31.4% sits at the 91st percentile age-matched for a 29-year-old male, so unlike the older female members the raw number and the percentile agree: it is genuinely high. And ALMI is at the 6th percentile, up from the 3rd, against the 60th Daniel named. Arc: BUILD FIRST, BURN SECOND. High body fat and low ALMI together is the one cell in the table where you say plainly that the muscle comes first and the leaning out follows it. He lost 5.39 lb of fat while adding 0.34 lb of lean in four weeks, which is why the taper up exists: the deficit was doing the fat-loss job and starving the muscle job. Goal is lbm-gain but path_source: almi, so it was derived from the numbers, not agreed with him. Never quote the ALMI kg/m² value.
Aesthetics and performance: a leaner athletic look, better Krav Maga, endurance for belt progression, improved posture, and a bodyweight pull-up. He already reports clothes fitting better, more energy in Krav sessions and better posture. why_strength 2, band anchored on identity and pursuit, 12 insights, so it can be used directly rather than hedged. His own August words: "I want to get rid of the excess unhealthy fat and build muscles for better aesthetics . Additonally want to build enough upper body strength to do pull ups . I will continue to gain more experience in Krav Maga .I need to work on my food as well - switch to sustainable meal prep ." From the Sep 2 room: long-term target 12 to 15% body fat, visible muscle, Krav Maga explosiveness and confidence, and "I can kill every 1 of you guys" is the identity payoff he is actually buying. Grandfather passed at 95 during the last block and he held consistency through it.

🚩 Coaching Points
  • Lean loss is the entire story and it has not stopped. 92.86 → 91.29 → 88.31 → 84.35. Last block took 3.96 lb of lean against 3.72 lb of fat, so 51% of the loss was the wrong tissue. ALMI 59th → 54th → 46th → 31st, down at every single scan.
  • She is 2 lb from her goal weight, so the scale has nothing left to give. Any further weight loss from here is overwhelmingly likely to be lean. This is the moment the conversation changes from losing to holding, and Monday is where to say it.
  • Dumbbell bench is going backwards while everything else climbs. Epley est 1RM 70 → 60 → 57 across Sep 11, 18 and 25, at a fixed 45. Her injury record says the left-quad spasm activates during bench, so ask before prescribing: is this the quad or is this strength? The answer changes what we do.
  • The RDL has a real ceiling and it is equipment, not effort. Parked at 70 since Sep 14 and she raised it herself on Sep 17: the Bowflex dumbbells are clunky, there are no safeties, and racking them after a heavy set frightens her. She is right to be careful with an L3 to L5 history. Solve the setup, do not push the load. The kickstand deadlift went 45 to 50 this week, so there is a hinge she is happy to load.
  • Food boredom is the adherence risk. No Cow, Owen bars, smoothies and vegan burgers on rotation, and she flagged it herself. Protein is short at about 116 g against a 140 target, and variety is the lever that moves it.
  • No food logging since Sep 22, seven days. Macros Journal total 0, photo journal silent. The 2-day task is live on Oct 3 and Oct 4 and Send 3 points at it.
  • Sep 23 session still carries 1 unread member comment. That session contains the RDL and the outstanding ask was a deadlift video. Neither the API nor UI clicks would open it this run. Read it before asking her for the video again, and nothing in this week’s drafts asks.
  • She takes a target the first time it is given. Asked on Sep 22 for Single Arm Row at 30, she trained it at 30 on Sep 28 and put three other lifts up in the same session. That is the single best argument for giving her more specific numbers, not fewer.
  • Nothing is mechanically owed to her. The last message in the thread is hers, “Thanks!” Sep 22 5:33 PM, a thank-you rather than a question. His question about the quad was left unanswered by her, not by us.
  • Effort is not the problem. 12 of 13 sessions, four rated “Difficult”, intensifiers being used, loads climbing on six lifts. If lean fell again it is a fueling decision we got wrong, not effort she withheld, and it should be owned that way.
  • Quad activation: not coach debt, but the loose end is now a week old. Re-verified absent from all six upcoming sessions including P3 (0 hits for activation, foam, couch or massage). Daniel ruled on 22 Sep that this was always hers to do and never a program change, and the live Sep 17 session note files it under Mobility as hers. But the 22 Sep DM told her “I will get a quad activation piece built into the front of your sessions”, so either build it into P3 or tell her plainly that the foam roller work is the whole of it. Nothing in this week’s drafts mentions it in any tense.
  • VAT is 88.0, above the 80 line, but falling at every scan (113.0 → 109.9 → 91.6 → 88.0) and the visceral-strategy box is unticked with her 10-day moment long past (Jun 10). Not a send and not a recommendation, just a number he may want to name: he told her on Sep 3 that under 80 was the next target and she is 8 away.
  • No renewal cliff, no close, no Slack post. 6 Month Core, Jun 29 to Dec 29 2026, Active. Routine package checkpoint. The renewal conversation belongs at the December scan.
  • She wants the mechanism, not the instruction. The leucine threshold explanation landed on Sep 17 precisely because it was an explanation. Teach, then prescribe.
🎯 Scan-Day Focus
  1. Frame the window before any number. 32 days, Sep 3 to Oct 5, and say plainly that the read is fat versus lean, not the scale.
  2. Open on the four months, not the four weeks. 21.5 lb down since May, 12.9 lb of it fat, body fat percentile 40th to 21st, visceral fat 113 to 88 and falling at every scan. That is a genuine transformation and it is hers.
  3. Then go straight at lean, and own it. 8.51 lb of lean gone since May and ALMI 59th to 31st. She trained 12 of 13 and rated four of them Difficult, so this is a fueling call we got wrong on a GLP-1, not effort she withheld.
  4. Say the thing about goal weight. She is 2 lb from the number she came in wanting. From here, more weight off is lean off. The job changes today from losing to holding, and she needs to hear that explicitly or she will keep chasing the scale.
  5. Protein is the lever, and name the gap. Her last complete day was about 116 g against 140. That is roughly one more solid feeding, not a diet change. Leucine or the EAAs cover the meals that land small.
  6. Fix the food boredom rather than repeating the number. She raised it herself. Two or three new items off the recipe list will move protein further than another reminder.
  7. Credit the Single Arm Row before anything else in the training conversation. She was given 30 and took 30 within a week, and put three more lifts up the same session. Name it.
  8. Ask about the bench before prescribing. Epley est 1RM 70 to 60 to 57 at a fixed 45. Her notes say the left quad fires during bench. Find out which it is, then decide.
  9. Ask his own unanswered question: how has the quad behaved over the last few workouts?
  10. Solve the deadlift setup, do not push the load. She is at her comfort ceiling on the Bowflex dumbbells with no safeties and an L3 to L5 history. Kickstand deadlift (already at 50), a trap-bar style hold, lighter with a slower eccentric, or a different hinge. Suggest, do not mandate.
  11. Close the quad activation loop one way or the other. She was told in writing on 22 Sep that it would be built into the front of her sessions. Either build it into P3 or tell her the foam roller work is the whole of it.
  12. Read the Sep 23 comment before the room, in case the deadlift video is already sitting in it.
  13. Ask the “what have you noticed” question live if she has not replied. Build prompts, not fat-loss prompts: shoulders and sleeves, fuller not smaller, weights lighter in the hand, how the back is holding up.
  14. Leave out: bone density (32 days is noise), any calorie target, the ALMI kg/m² value, and the bench, squat and deadlift Gold Standards.
  15. No renewal question, no close, no Slack post. Routine package checkpoint inside a term running to Dec 29, 2026.

  • Lean mass gain. ALMI at the 15th percentile is the whole reason this is a build. Body fat is the 48th age-matched percentile, which is average for a 51-year-old man, so there is no burn signal here and fat-loss framing would be wrong.
  • Why: longevity and aesthetics for his family and his four kids. Turning 50 in control, his own words “level 50” and “savage mode”, with visible abs like Jake Gyllenhaal in Road House. He turned 51 on Sep 22 and you already sent him “after 40 we only count in levels, not years. Level 51 sounds way better!”
  • This block, since the Sep 22 call: upper body push / pull / arms plus core, 3x a week. No leg work. Protein 212, carbs 225 on training days and 150 on rest, fats around 80. Three fist-sized protein meals, two shakes at 1.5 scoops, 1.5 cups Greek yogurt at night. No avocado. Carbs stacked around training, Rice Krispies or cream of rice in the post-workout shake.
  • Why strength is genuine, not generic. why_strength score 2, band “anchored”, anchored on person and identity, 22 insights. Safe to reflect back.
  • Goal source is his numbers, not a conversation. path_source almi, “ALMI 15th, well under 60th”. Treat lbm-gain as a working assumption he has not explicitly signed off.

Gold Standards — Female, 60+ · 215.0 lb (Scan 1 bodyweight)
BB Bench—~160 lb 1RM (0.75x BW)
BB Squat—~270 lb 1RM (1.25x BW)

Do NOT put the Gold Standards in front of her on Monday. Two reasons, and both of them bite here. The multipliers key off bodyweight, so at 215 lb hers are inflated and they will fall as she leans out, which is the opposite of the signal we want for a fat-loss member. And she is four weeks into her first structured program, at 61, training at home on adjustable dumbbells with a sensitive lower back and no barbell anywhere in the plan. 🚩 Coaching Points
  • RESOLVED: the return block is built. Yesterday’s P1 said the program ended Oct 1 with nothing past it. Live this run: Prime | P1 | A on Oct 4, then Recomp | Dumbbells | Full Body A/B/C three times a week from Oct 5 to Nov 6, with P2 versions from Nov 2. Nothing is owed.
  • But the whole return block is dumbbells only, and she has a full gym. Courtside gives her machines, some barbell and free weights. Her stated focus is intensity to protect ALMI, her barbell squat has been parked at 60 since Aug 22, and the new block has no barbell, machine or cable work in it anywhere. He promised 3 full-body sessions and delivered exactly that, so this is a choice to confirm rather than a miss. Ask the question before Monday.
  • ALMI 23rd to 20th percentile in five weeks, and all of the lean came off her arms. Arms lean 8.9 to 8.3, legs and trunk flat. The arm is on deliberate rest, so some of that is a trade we chose. Own it as a decision rather than letting her read it as failure, and say what the next block does about it.
  • Three identical completed sets, still. He has taught this at length twice, 10 Sep and 14 Sep. Today’s session came back RDL 60 × 10, 10, 10 and single arm row 25 × 10, 10, 10, the fourth session of identical sets on that row. The one bright spot is the Arnold press: 35 × 9 then 26 × 10, which is exactly right. Lead with what she got right, then ask whether this is not landing or whether the elbow is capping it.
  • The left elbow is not improving and it is shaping the numbers. Dumbbell bent over row went 60 on Sep 14 to 40 on Sep 21. She told him on 14 Sep it was not getting better and asked for full rest; he agreed 15 Sep and again 26 Sep. A tennis elbow band made it worse. The in-person form assessment on Oct 22, 11:15 AM is where grip and wrist position get looked at.
  • Knees do not like the hack squat angle. Her words, 14 Sep. He gave the foot-forward cue on 15 Sep. Leg press progression is slow for the same reason. One question in the room, then decide. Suggest, do not mandate: name the variable (foot position, angle, load), never drop a movement class.

  • The 58% is misleading and he should not open with it. Six of the eight misses in the last 30 days are the secondary Core/Care program, which ended 18 Sep and which he himself told her on 16 Sep she could move or skip. On the primary plan she is 11 of 13 in 30 days and 13 of 15 since Aug 24.

  • VAT is 114.2 and the visceral-strategy box is unticked, with her 10-day moment long past (19 Aug). Above the 80 line, but falling hard, and reducing visceral fat is already her own stated focus. Not a send and not a recommendation, just a number he may want to name.

🎯 Scan-Day Focus
  1. Frame the window first. 26 days, Sep 9 to Oct 5, and nearly two weeks of it abroad. Say plainly that the read is lean versus fat, not the scale.
  2. Open on visceral fat and on her steps. 126.2 to 114.2 cm², and 15,170 steps a day averaged across the last week while travelling. Both are hers and neither shows up on her bathroom scale, which is the thing she said was not showing her anything.
  3. Then say the thing about 128 lb. She is 137.2 with lean already down a pound. From here, more weight off is lean off. The job changes today from losing to building, and she needs to hear it in those words.
  4. Go at the ALMI number and own the trade. 23rd to 20th percentile, and every ounce of the lean came off the arms. Part of that is a rest we chose for the elbow. Name it as a decision, then say what the next block does about it. Her long-term marker is the 70th percentile, set at intro, and she remembers it.
  5. Credit the Arnold press before criticising the rest. Sep 29: 35 for 9, then 26 for 10. That is the pattern he has asked for three times, done unprompted, in a hotel gym. Then ask why the row is still 25 for 10, 10, 10 four sessions running.
  6. Carbs, not protein. Protein has been said three times. Carbs around training have not, and at 104 to 135 g against this activity level they are the reason hard sets feel harder than they should. This is also the natural moment for the pre/post workout nutrition strategy, which has never been sent. No calorie number.
  7. Settle the equipment question out loud. She is home with a full gym, and the block he has built for the next five weeks is dumbbells only. If that is deliberate, tell her why. If it is not, this is the moment to change it.
  8. Ask about the elbow before prescribing anything upper body. It has not improved across two calls, it has cost her 20 on the bent over row, and the form assessment is not until Oct 22.
  9. Ask about the knees and the hack squat angle. One question, then decide. Suggest a variable, do not drop a movement.
  10. Close the referral question she asked on 16 Sep.
  11. Ask the “what have you noticed” question live if she has not replied. Recomp prompts: looser in some places and tighter in others, the scale barely moving while clothes change, how her legs and back held up at 15,000 steps a day abroad.
  12. Leave out: bone density (26 days is noise), any calorie target, the ALMI kg/m² value, the bench, squat and deadlift Gold Standards, and any phase-transition announcement.
  13. No renewal question, no close, no Slack post. Routine package checkpoint inside a term running to Feb 3, 2027.
